### INT-393 · A trade counter keeps its takings in a till of its own (B18-DP-01, ECON-P3)
- **Trigger:** a sale by the tavernkeeper at the tavern's counter or a keeper at a skilled-trade counter (`World.trade`), and every morning (`Business.daily`).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/business_money.gd::after_trade`, `game/scripts/core/business_money.gd::counter_building`, `game/scripts/core/business_money.gd::daily`, `game/scripts/core/business_money.gd::_settle`, `game/scripts/core/business_money.gd::counter_owner`, `game/scripts/core/business.gd::tills`
- **Affects:** Coins, Households, Market, Save, Wages
- **Expected:** The coins of a counter sale go into the building's `counter.till`, not the keeper's purse (counted in `World.total_coins` through `Business.tills`). Each morning the same rule as the households' storefronts (`_settle`): the payee (the household that holds the building, else the keeper who last sold) draws the shortfall of their necessity reserve first, is paid `payout_share` of what the till holds above `business_money.till_reserve`, and tops up a bare till only from spendable coins; with nobody left the till goes to the manor. A sale made away from the counter stays in the seller's purse. A save without the record reads as an empty till. The payee is one rule, `BusinessMoney.counter_owner`, which the economy model reads as the till's controller (`EconActor` "counter:<building id>").
- **Tests:** `game/tests/business_money_test.gd::_counters`, `game/tests/till_ownership_test.gd::test_counter`

### INT-400 · A craftsman works the recipe that returns most under local prices (WDR-012-C)
- **Trigger:** A tanner or carpenter choosing a recipe (`NPCBrain._crafter`) and a smith choosing what to forge (`NPCBrain._smith`).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::recipe_return`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::by_return`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_crafter`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_smith`
- **Affects:** Market, Crafting, Employment
- **Expected:** Among the recipes the craftsman can make now, the one whose products fetch most less its inputs at the local asking price (`World.sell_price`, `World.sell_price_at` with `World.local_supply`: dearer as the maker's own stock runs low, cheaper among many local sellers; the prices the player meets) goes first; equal returns keep the old order, so the choice is deterministic. A smith's recipes made for a buyer who wants them (jewellery, helmet, nails, shovel blades) still come before goods for stock (PAR-04). The stock caps and the rules for who may make what are unchanged. Nothing is made or lost; the stat `spec_switches` counts the choices that differ from the old fixed order. Provisional: no new balance value.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/competition_test.gd`

### INT-401 · The manor raises its hands' wage when its places stay unfilled (WDR-012-C)
- **Trigger:** Each morning's `NPCBrain.seek_work`, and the wage paid by `pay_guards`.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_hiring_competition`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::manor_hand_wage`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::job_market`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::pay_guards`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::to_dict`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::load_dict`
- **Affects:** Wages, Employment, Treasury, Save
- **Expected:** When the manor has affordable places open and villagers who looked for work did not take them for `hiring_competition.unfilled_days` mornings, the hands' day wage rises by `raise_step`, up to `raise_max` above `manor_wage_per_day` (provisional); filled for as many mornings it eases a step. The wage is paid to every hand from the treasury as before (`gov_wages`), counts in `daily_obligations`, and the manor takes on only as many hands as its treasury can pay at that wage (`job_market`), so no coin is made. Seekers weigh the offer by the ordinary job-switch rule (margin and minimum gain). Saved as `wage_offers` (absent: no raise). Other employers' places (construction helpers, business attendants) keep their flat wages.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/competition_test.gd`

### INT-402 · A failing merchant moves to a better stall or leaves the trade (WDR-012-C)
- **Trigger:** Each morning's `NPCBrain.seek_work` for a merchant whose household is not living on its income.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_relocate_stall`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::stall_value`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::seek_work`, `game/scripts/core/property.gd::relet_stalls`
- **Affects:** Market, Employment, Property
- **Expected:** Before looking for other work, a failing NPC merchant moves to a vacant manor stall of the same kind where the goods in hand would fetch at least `job_switch_margin` times as much at the asking prices there (`World.local_supply` around that stall: fewer rival sellers within the radius, a dearer price); the old stall falls vacant and is let again by `relet_stalls`. With no such stall the merchant looks for other work as any villager, leaving the trade if a place pays clearly more, and the stall falls vacant; a merchant nobody takes work for keeps failing (no rescue). The player is never moved and gets no preference: the same prices apply to their stall.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/competition_test.gd`

### INT-403 · The music and ambience follow the hour, the place and a fight; effects follow the player's actions (MUSIC-18, SFX-18)
- **Trigger:** Every frame (`Main._audio_step`): the game hour, the depth the player is on, the title screen, a fight near the player, the player outside the village map, the season; and the player's own actions (a swing, a landed blow, a purchase, a chest opened, gear worn, a healing item used, a doorway, a quest, a skill level, a rest, a save, a stroke of chopping, mining or picking, steps walked).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/audio.gd::slot_for`, `game/scripts/core/audio.gd::ambience_for`, `game/scripts/core/audio.gd::play_sfx`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_audio_step`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_in_fight`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_footsteps`, `game/scripts/view/door_view.gd::update`
- **Affects:** Interface, Calendar, Overworld, Combat
- **Expected:** Audio only reads: the world's state, clock and random numbers are never written (the effects' variants and pitch use the audio node's own generator), so a run is identical with the sound on or off and in a headless run. Music, first match: the title (morning song), a fight (Fair Fight; a bandit or anyone fighting the player within 12 cells, a wild animal angry at the player; held 6 s), the countryside beyond the village (Plain Sight), below the surface (Misted Ancient), else morning 06-10, day 10-18, night 18-06. Ambience layers (at most two, cross-faded) follow the hour, the depth, the countryside and the season. Music plays on the Music bus and ambience and effects on the SFX bus, so the settings sliders act at once. Without an audio device nothing is loaded or played.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/audio_test.gd`

### INT-404 · The chapel gives what is left of the offerings as alms to the poorest households (CHAPEL-R2)
- **Trigger:** The weekly service day, 16:00, after the offerings and the clergy's wages (`Chapel.evening`).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/chapel.gd::alms`, `game/scripts/core/chapel.gd::household_shortfall`, `game/scripts/core/chapel.gd::evening`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::necessity`
- **Affects:** Coins, Households, Ledger
- **Expected:** (owner CHAPEL-R2, 2026-10-23, Priest + alms: "offerings keep the priest, and what is left goes as alms to the poorest households"; reserve and distribution rules chosen by the owner) The fund keeps the wages owed to the clergy and seven days of their wages; the rest goes to households whose necessity (`World.necessity`, OD-19/PAR-02A: the household's foreseeable food, fuel, medicine and rent less income, the same for every member) exceeds the coins all their members hold, each up to that shortfall, the largest shortfall first (ties by household index), paid once per household to its lowest-id adult (else elder). What is left stays in the fund; the fund never falls below what it keeps; coins move between named purses only. The player is not a household and is not given alms (the player may ask for food, INT-226). Food relief (INT-226) and wages (INT-225) are unchanged.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/chapel_test.gd::test_alms`, `game/tests/chapel_test.gd::test_wages`, `game/tests/conservation_test.gd`

### INT-405 · A migrant is the same person on the way back: leaving and returning keep the id, the record and every tie (ID-01)
- **Trigger:** A person leaves the village for a settlement (`Migration._gone`) and, later, a settlement member of that id comes to the village (`Migration._villager`, `Migration._settled`); a settlement member is born (`OffMapSettlement._try_birth`) or dies.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/settlement/migration.gd::_gone`, `game/scripts/core/settlement/migration.gd::_villager`, `game/scripts/core/settlement/migration.gd::_reactivate`, `game/scripts/core/settlement/migration.gd::_arrived`, `game/scripts/core/identity.gd::move`, `game/scripts/core/settlement/offmap.gd::_try_birth`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::offmap_person`
- **Affects:** Family, Settlements, Save, Households
- **Expected:** (owner ID-01, 2026-10-23: "Migration must transfer the existing person, not create a replacement") The person's id is the villager's id for good: the settlement member carries it (sixth entry), `World.people` says where they are (the village, a settlement, dead, the player, an ancestor) and is never pruned, and the two counters (`World.next_person_id`, the village's, below 1,000,000; `World.next_band_id`, settlements and player characters, from 1,000,000) only rise, so an id is never issued twice and the ranges cannot meet. On leaving, the villager's record stays (alive false, `away` the settlement); on returning, that same record is reactivated (alive, `away` cleared, and the age, skills, purse and goods they come back with), so every tie (friends, regard), memory, family link and civic reference keyed by the id is there again; their household, home and job are found as for any arrival. A person born in a settlement has an id from the settlement band and keeps it (their first villager record is made under that id when they arrive). A death in a settlement marks the register. No coins or goods are made or lost (the books of the move are unchanged); a move that falls through puts the person back as they were.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/identity_test.gd::test_leave_and_return`, `game/tests/identity_test.gd::test_two_settlements_twice`, `game/tests/identity_test.gd::test_save_mid_journey`, `game/tests/identity_test.gd::test_offmap_birth_then_arrival`, `game/tests/identity_test.gd::test_death_in_settlement`, `game/tests/migration_test.gd`

### INT-406 · An heir keeps their own identity while the village's view of the player goes on (ID-01, ID-D1)
- **Trigger:** The player dies and the family continues (`World.continue_as_heir`, `Life.make_heir`, `Identity.succeed`).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/world.gd::continue_as_heir`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::make_heir`, `game/scripts/core/identity.gd::succeed`
- **Affects:** Family, Save, Coins
- **Expected:** (owner ID-D1, 2026-10-23: keep Build 18's family-reputation continuity as the baseline, but the heir keeps their own permanent identity and the predecessor and heir stay separate people) The villager ties kept under the player's id (-2) go on to the heir exactly as in Build 18 (no new reputation formula). The player's own child who becomes the heir keeps their id and their villager record (not alive, `became_player`, empty-handed: what they carried is in the loot), so their own ties, history and links stay theirs and the id is never issued again; every player character has a person id (`PlayerState.pid`); the predecessor becomes an ancestor in the register under their own id, with a read-only snapshot of the ties they had at death (`World.tie_history`, read by no rule). Links to a former player (-3) stay as they are. Nothing is counted twice and no coins change.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/identity_test.gd::test_succession_child`, `game/tests/identity_test.gd::test_succession_relative`, `game/tests/aging_test.gd::test_player`, `game/tests/succession_test.gd`

### INT-407 · Judgment is on: what villagers see, do and are told becomes their opinion of a person (X19-SCOPE)
- **Trigger:** Any witnessed or suffered deed the live game already knows (a theft seen, an attack, a killing, a gift, help, a rescue, a kept or broken contract or debt, a robbery, a marriage), and every hour two villagers spend together.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_attack`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_killing`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_deal`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::enabled`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::learn`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::tell`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::net`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::setup`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::load_dict`
- **Affects:** Judgment, Crime, Market, Employment, Relief, Family, Combat, Save
- **Expected:** (owner X19-SCOPE, 2026-10-23: Build 19 ships the complete Expansion 19, all enabled) `judgment.json` `enabled` is true, so every new world and every loaded save (a Build 18 save has no `judgment` section and starts with a clean, empty store on the loaded day) keeps a `JudgmentStore`. Each deed becomes a fact; each person who sees or takes part judges it for themselves (victim, friend or kin of the victim, kin or friend of the actor, a rival of the victim, a bystander read it differently); gossip carries it between people who spend time together, at the closeness chance (`gossip.closeness_floor_permille` 500, certain at the top of regard), believed by the teller's trust (kept apart from liking). Ordinary judgments fade with a half-life of 16 game days (`decay.ordinary_permille_per_day` 42, X19-DECAY); a killing and an assault are permanent. The weighing is one factor inside the existing decision points (`JudgmentWeighting.choose`: sell to, buy from, hire, take work, befriend, court, accept a contract, lend, barter, ask for food, give toward a home, and the family decisions); feasibility, coins and items are never overridden, so conservation is untouched, and every random draw is a hash of the world seed and the permanent ids (never `World.rng`, so no other random stream moves). The `World` writes a `judgment` section (version 2) and reads versions 1 and 2; a section it cannot read is kept and written back. `JudgmentLive.test_disabled` is the Build 18 path (no store; a saved section carried untouched).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/judgment_test.gd`, `game/tests/judgment_live_test.gd`, `game/tests/judgment_guard_save_test.gd`, `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_save_and_upgrade`

### INT-408 · Rules for what counts as an attack, a killing and a fair deal (X19-EVENT-Q)
- **Trigger:** A blow by the player or a villager (`World.raise_alarm`, `NPCBrain.fight`), a villager's death by a blow (`World.kill`), a debt repaid late (`Credit.repay`).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::note_blow`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::struck_first`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_attack`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_killing`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_deal`, `game/scripts/core/credit.gd::repay`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::fight`
- **Affects:** Judgment, Crime, Combat, Coins
- **Expected:** (owner 2026-10-23, section A "Approve all") (1) a guard killing an attacker is not published as a killing; (2) no feed records a first meeting as a fact; (3) a debt repaid late is published as a fair deal tagged `late`, with half the trust gain of one repaid on time; (4) self-defence: the first blow of a fight makes its aggressor (a fight ends when no blow has fallen for `self_defence.fight_minutes`), an answering blow in the same fight is not published as an assault, and killing the one who struck first is not published as a killing. Judgment only: the guards' own response (`raise_alarm`), fines and the coins that move are unchanged.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_event_rules`, `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_world_hooks`

### INT-409 · Villagers sometimes lie, and are found out (X19-MISINFO)
- **Trigger:** A conversation (`JudgmentStore.tell`, from `Relations.share`) in which a teller who has a motive against someone passes on a real story about that person.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::tell`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::_maybe_lie`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::_invent`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::_refute`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::lie_motive`
- **Affects:** Judgment, Crime
- **Expected:** (owner 2026-10-23, section D) Only someone with serious resentment toward the subject (`lies.min_resentment`) or a rivalry (their NET of the subject at their rival line) may invent a story, at most one per liar per game year, in `lies.chance_permille` (50, 5%) of the meetings in which they gossip about that person, by a seeded hash (never `World.rng`). The story is a fact tagged `["lie", liar]` accusing the subject of a theft or a cheat; the liar holds it but judges nobody by it; the listener believes it as any told story. A guard who hears it, or kin of the accused, shows it false: the listener never takes it up, a teller who had taken it up takes back what they judged, and the liar loses `trust.rumour_false` (15 points) of trust with each who learns. Everyone else keeps believing until told. Since Expansion 20 (R1, INT-520) the refutation is itself a fact, `false_accusation`, that travels and corrects those who later learn it. A lie is never handed down as a family story. The reference `gossip.timing` "hour" never invents. No item or coin is involved.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_lies`, `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_lie_frequency`

### INT-410 · Parents hand down the deeds they cannot forget (X19-STORY-LIFE)
- **Trigger:** A child comes of age (`Life._set_role`), then each hour until the parents have told them or four game days pass (`World._on_hour`).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_come_of_age`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::tell_family_tick`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::tell_family_stories`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::keep_forever`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::_set_role`
- **Affects:** Judgment, Family
- **Expected:** (owner 2026-10-23, section D) When the new adult and a living parent are inside the new adult's home together, the parent tells them the permanent deeds they hold (a killing, an assault, a betrayal by a friend: a betrayal memory is made permanent when it is recorded) and the serious good deeds (`family_story.good_severity`, a rescue), each passing at the gossip closeness chance; ordinary deeds fade and are not passed on, and an invented story never is. The child judges each for themselves with the parent as teller. The pending entry is saved (`stories`) and lapses after `family_story.expire_days`.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_family_stories`, `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_world_hooks`

### INT-411 · Personality and stubbornness shape how a villager decides (X19-PERS-FORMULA, X19-TRAIT-DIST)
- **Trigger:** A firsthand deed that touches a villager (as victim of a bad one or beneficiary of a good one), a costly mistake the game measures (a robbery's loss, a beast's bite, a meal missed with food to hand, a favour refused), a reminder, and any weighed decision.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_personality.gd::experience`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_personality.gd::mistake`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_personality.gd::stubborn_weight`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_weighting.gd::choose`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::costly_mistake`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::tolerance`
- **Affects:** Judgment, Employment, Market, Family
- **Expected:** (owner 2026-10-23, section B "Approve") Tolerance differs by person (up to 30%), is pulled by firsthand provocation and help by 2% a severity point (more for repeats within a game year and for lasting hardship, less the further from where the person started: a fifth of the force at 40% away, never 0; recovery at full rate, growth past the origin at half), and the trait formula replaces the old SJD-Q13 step for the same event (RECON-01). About 30% of villagers can be stubborn (a hash of their id), starting at 20-60%; wisdom grows 2% a cost point (repeats add half again, at most 3 a year; a quarter carries to every situation; tops out at 50%) and reduces stubbornness's weight by that share; anger is 10% a severity point fading evenly over a game day, a reminder (the offender met or the story retold) restores a quarter of the peak (never above half of it), and at half or more the stubborn override holds for the anger's remaining time. In a weighed decision a stubborn chooser's options that deal with a resented person or give up their last choice in that decision carry a penalty of stubbornness x (1 - wisdom) (`traits.stubborn`, provisional sizes), and under the override both are decisive. Non-holders are untouched. All draws are hashes of the world seed and ids; the last-choice record and fights are saved.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/judgment_personality_test.gd`, `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_stubbornness_in_decisions`, `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_clock_and_anger`

### INT-412 · A family's name colours how others see a person, never alone a refusal (X19-SPILL-REACH, X19-REP-TRANSMIT, X19-BOND-LIMITS)
- **Trigger:** Any weighed decision about a person (`JudgmentWeighting.choose`), and any regard gain between two people (`Relations`).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_bonds.gd::_extended`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_bonds.gd::family_reputation`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::colour`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::disposition`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_weighting.gd::choose`
- **Affects:** Judgment, Family, Market, Employment
- **Expected:** (owner 2026-10-23, section C "Wider reach" and the clarification on cousins; the ring weights are PROVISIONAL agent values) Relatives by relationship distance over the parent, child and partner links: ring 1 parents, children, siblings, spouse (spillover 30%, a good name in full); ring 2 grandparents, grandchildren, aunts, uncles, nieces, nephews, in-laws (15%, half); ring 3 first cousins (7.5%, a quarter); ring 4 second cousins (3.75%, an eighth). The observer's impression is the sum of their own remembered judgments of those relatives (no new store; they fade with X19-DECAY); each dealing with the person replaces a tenth of it; it never weighs more than a quarter of the strongest personal judgment the observer holds; and when the weighing ends in a refusal that the observer's own judgment of the person would not have made, it is made again without the family name (`colour_refusal_blocked`). It is not a judgment of the person: NET and trust are untouched, and the direct, relationship-weighted reaction to a witnessed deed (`interpret`) is a separate path. It also feeds the +5% regard-gain bonus for a positive family name (`JudgmentBonds.gain_permille`), still before Relations' caps.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_reach`, `game/tests/judgment_x19_test.gd::test_colour_and_limits`, `game/tests/judgment_bonds_test.gd`

### INT-413 · A parent shields the family from a bandit or a beast (FAM-SAC-01 danger, "Shield and fetch help")
- **Trigger:** An awake head of household with no plan to follow (`NPCBrain.decide`), a bandit or a wolf or bear that is fighting someone within `decision.family.danger.radius` of a dependant who is out of doors.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_family.gd::knows_killer`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_family.gd::danger`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_family.gd::_shield`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_family.gd::_fight`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_family.gd::_threats`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::decide`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_report`
- **Affects:** Judgment, Family, Combat, Crime, Bandits, Wildlife
- **Expected:** (owner 2026-10-23, answers on family danger and the hierarchy children, spouse, parents, self) The head weighs `family_danger` through `JudgmentWeighting.choose` with the family-priority factors recorded in `JudgmentStore.family_trace`: shield (the dependants out of doors are sent home; the head steps between the threat and the nearest; for a bandit the head files a report that the ordinary path (PAR-05) carries to a guard, who then takes on the bandit), fight (a bandit is made the head's `target` and the ordinary fight runs; a beast is struck when within reach, and turns on the head), or flee (only below the survival floor of health, or the rare self-first temperament, recorded). Cornered raises fight, as does stubbornness in proportion to health; the per-person weight spread bends readiness, never the order of the family. The head can be hurt or killed in the ordinary fight; loot and meat from a beast the head kills are made as for the player. Fire has no hook until a fire exists. Off with judgment off (Build 18). (A7, GP-CRIME-PURSUIT) A player with a killing in their case (`Justice`) who is out is a threat too, met only by a head who knows who it is (`JudgmentFamily.knows_killer`: saw it, heard the hue and cry, or believes the killing): shielding reports the sighting to a guard (`World.report_crime`, how "sighting"); fighting takes the player on (`NPC.target_ref`).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/family_first_test.gd::test_danger`, `game/tests/manhunt_test.gd::test_family_fears_killer`

### INT-414 · A guest's action is applied by the host, as that guest (MP Batch A2)
- **Trigger:** A guest machine joined to a host over the LAN (`NetSession.join`, `c_hello`) walks (`c_move`) or asks for an action (`NetSession.request`: eating from the pack, a tapped wild plant).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::c_hello`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::guest_state`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::c_move`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::apply_requests`, `game/scripts/net/net_actions.gd::apply`, `game/scripts/net/net_actions.gd::apply_patch`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::as_actor`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::active_players`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::set_local_player`, `game/scripts/main.gd::start_guest_world`, `game/scripts/main.gd::guest_blocked`
- **Affects:** Player state, Inventory, Food, Save, Interface, Ledger
- **Expected:**
  - The host's World is the only simulation. A guest is a full player of `World.players` by a permanent pid (`Identity.register_player`), a newcomer on its first visit (MP-D7: the starting kit, its purse counted in as `newcomer_coins_in` and the kit as `imported_`), found again by the hash of its device key (`PlayerState.net_key`, never the key); the same key already playing is refused, as are another version, a wrong password and a full world.
  - A guest's moves are accepted up to the sprint speed onto open ground on its own level, else it is sent back. Its requests queue on arrival and are applied once each at one fixed point of the host's frame (before `World.update`), in arrival order, by the same World verbs as the host's (`World.player_eat`, `World.player_reach_cell`, `World.pick_plant`) run as the guest (`World.as_actor`); the second of two players reaching for one plant finds it taken. The answer carries the guest's character as the host has it and the changed records; every second the host sends each guest its own needs, pack and purse.
  - A guest's machine shows the host's world from its own character (`World.set_local_player`): the other players' public records only (no pack, purse, crimes or identity), never simulated or saved; actions the host does not take from guests yet do nothing there and say so.
  - A guest who leaves goes out of active play (`PlayerState.away`, MP-D1): no needs, no wear, not a detail centre; every record stays, in the host's save (21, optional keys `net_key`, `away`). In a session with guests the host's menu or fall does not stop the world (MP-D3). Single player has no guest: nothing of this runs.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/net_g1_test.gd`, `game/tests/multiplayer_a1_test.gd`

### INT-415 · The authorities' case on an offender (GP-CRIME-PURSUIT A1)
- **Trigger:** A player commits a crime (a theft, a pocket picked, fencing, livestock, a lockpicking attempt, a blow, a blow at a guard, a killing) or a guard learns of one.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::record`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::learn`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::tier_of`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::settle`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::dawn`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::on_death`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::crime_record`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::witness`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_assault`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::_player_killing`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::raise_alarm`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::report_crime`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::deliver_report`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::guard_confront`, `game/scripts/core/relations.gd::notoriety`, `game/data/balance.json`
- **Affects:** Crime, Combat, Save, Player state
- **Expected:** One case per offender, keyed by the player's reference ("player", "player:<pid>"; never -2), extending `PlayerState.crimes` (which now also holds "assault", "guard_assault" and "killing"). Each crime in the case points at its record entry. Severity is the judgment catalog's (theft 1000, assault 2000, killing 3000); the tier a guard acts on is that of the outstanding crimes that guard knows (minor, theft, repeated_theft, assault, murder, multiple_murder; a killing answered by a fine or by being taken in still counts toward multiple murder). A guard knows a crime only by seeing it, being told (a report now carries the reference and the crimes), the hue and cry, or another guard's word (INT-417); an unseen crime is in the case and known to no guard. A fine paid settles the theft; at dawn an outstanding crime past its tier's `wanted_days` lapses (murder never); the offender's death closes the case (owner answer 3: the stain is the family's reputation, JudgmentBonds). Notoriety ignores the new violent kinds (killings are already counted). Saved as the optional World key "justice" (a save 21 has none). Numbers: balance.json "justice".
- **Tests:** `game/tests/justice_test.gd`

### INT-416 · A guard on a case pursues, searches and keeps the offender wanted (GP-CRIME-PURSUIT A2)
- **Trigger:** A guard goes on a player's case (saw the crime, was told, heard the hue and cry, met a guard on it, or sees a wanted player again), or loses sight of them.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::engage`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::search`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::begin_search`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::release`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::spot`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::search_cells`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::search_until`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::fight`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_pursuit_sight`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::pursue`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::pursue_arrived`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_guard`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::sees`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::_update_npc`, `game/scripts/core/npc.gd::to_dict`
- **Affects:** Crime, Combat, Save
- **Expected:** `NPC.pursuit` {ref, state, last-known place and minute, places to search}: pursuing while the guard sees the player (`World.sees`, the one channel of sight), with the S3 path-following round walls and no 25-tile give-up; out of sight, searching the last-known place and then nearby hiding places and ways out (up to the tier's `search_points`), until `search_minutes` after the authorities last saw the player; then released, the case wanted (`Justice.status`). A guard told of a crime who cannot see the player responds to the reported place and searches there. A guard on the rounds who sees a wanted player they know takes up the chase again (`Justice.spot`, at decision time, never a scan). The authorities' last-known place moves only by a sighting or a report; nobody tracks the player. Saved: a guard's pursuit (optional key; a guard chasing in sight at the save is chasing again on load), the case (INT-415). Bandits and guards chasing bandits keep SIM-F4 unchanged.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/pursuit_test.gd`, `game/tests/sim_probes.gd::manhunt`

### INT-417 · The hue and cry, the bell and guards sharing a case (GP-CRIME-PURSUIT A3)
- **Trigger:** A killing is seen; a guard is struck; the player attacks again with the guards after them; a second killing becomes known; a guard on a case meets another guard; a villager who knows the offender sees them.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::hue_and_cry`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::escalate`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::share_tick`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::share`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::sighting`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::_player_killing`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_assault`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::decide`, `game/scripts/core/recorder/world_recorder.gd::occurrence`
- **Affects:** Crime, Combat, Judgment
- **Expected:** (owner answer 1, 2026-10-24: the approved exception to PAR-05's no-unsupported-sensing rule) Everyone awake within `justice.hue_and_cry_tiles` (PROPOSED 30) of the cry, on that level, indoors or out, hears it: guards learn the crimes and the place and come (or take the player on if they see them); villagers learn who is cried after (the case's aware list and a memory "heard the hue and cry: <name> killed <victim>"). A second known killing rings the bell, heard within `justice.bell_tiles` (PROPOSED 80). A guard on a case shares crimes and the newer last-known place with the guards in its sight at most every `share_minutes`; a free guard who then knows of an assault or worse joins, unless the trail is older than the tier's search time. Only the village's guards come; none is made. A villager in the aware list who sees the offender reports the sighting (at most every `sighting_minutes`), through PAR-05's walk to a guard. Bounded: one look round a minute per guard on a case.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/manhunt_test.gd`

### INT-418 · Yielding and being taken in: fine, goods returned, case settled (GP-CRIME-PURSUIT A3)
- **Trigger:** A beaten player with a guard of their case in sight yields (the first interaction choice), or a guard who may not kill beats them down; or a guard who knows of violence meets a theft.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::yield_guard`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::take_in`, `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::lethal`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_yield`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::damage_player`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::guard_confront`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::fight`, `game/scripts/main.gd::interact`, `game/scripts/core/recorder/world_recorder.gd::guard_begin`, `game/scripts/core/recorder/world_recorder.gd::guard_end`
- **Affects:** Crime, Combat, Treasury, Coins, Inventory, Interface
- **Expected:** (owner answer 2) A guard may not kill for theft or assault, nor for a single murder without resistance: their blows stop at `justice.subdue_hp` and then they take the player in. Lethal force: a murderer who struck a guard or attacked again with a guard on the case, and any multiple murderer. Yielded or taken in: the stolen goods carried go back to their owners, the player pays the tier's `yield_fine` plus the theft fine for the goods (what they have) to the treasury through the recorded fine path (coins move, none is made), every outstanding crime is settled, every guard leaves the case, and the villagers fighting them stand down. The morality ledgers are untouched. A guard who knows of an assault or worse does not settle a theft with a fine: they take the player on.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/manhunt_test.gd::test_take_in_and_yield`, `game/tests/manhunt_test.gd::test_lethal`, `game/tests/sim_probes.gd::manhunt`

### INT-419 · Village news as facts that travel by knowledge (GP-DIALOGUE A5)
- **Trigger:** A household buys, leases or fences land; the manor or the chapel feeds a household; a villager lacks the tool of their trade; anyone dies.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_news`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::on_death`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::publish`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::learn`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::_tell_news`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_store.gd::_bound_news`, `game/scripts/core/property.gd::buy`, `game/scripts/core/property.gd::lease`, `game/scripts/core/farm_plots.gd`, `game/scripts/core/chapel.gd::ask`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_note_relief`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_get_tool`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::kill`, `game/data/judgment.json`
- **Affects:** Judgment, Households, Property, Relief, Health
- **Expected:** NEUTRAL facts of family "news" (land_bought, land_leased, fenced, relief, no_tool, death), known to the actor and, for a household's affair, its members (participants), to those awake nearby (witnesses), and to others only by word of mouth. A death is known to its household and close family and those who see the body, by its kind only (slain, starvation, old age, other); the killer is known only from the killing itself (the household memory is "<name> died (slain)"). News is never judged (no ledger, trust, personality or bond moves), is held apart from the moral memories (`memory.news_capacity`) and told apart in the same meetings (`gossip.news_per_meeting`, `news_daily_budget`, its own seeded roll), with ids from `JudgmentStore.NEWS_BASE`: the moral gossip, its ids and its rolls are as they were (G0 identical with news live).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/dialogue_perspective_test.gd::test_news_not_judged`, `game/tests/dialogue_perspective_test.gd::test_lambert_facts`, `game/tests/dialogue_perspective_test.gd::test_oakley_facts`

### INT-420 · Telling: related events as one, the gravest first, nothing said over and over (GP-DIALOGUE A6)
- **Trigger:** The player asks a villager for the news or about home, or opens a conversation with a witness.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/event_talk.gd::groups`, `game/scripts/core/event_talk.gd::news_tier`, `game/scripts/core/event_talk.gd::mark_said`, `game/scripts/core/event_talk.gd::all_said`, `game/scripts/ui/dialogue_voice.gd::group_line`, `game/scripts/ui/dialogue.gd::news`, `game/scripts/ui/dialogue.gd::opening`, `game/scripts/ui/dialogue.gd::_home_news`, `game/scripts/core/npc.gd::to_dict`, `game/data/dialogue_events.json`
- **Affects:** Interface, Judgment, Households, Save
- **Expected:** The data's merge rules join related events (same household, place, giver or victim, within the rule's days) in the order they happened, every fact kept. Priority: the listener with the speaker's own people, then serious harm to their own people, the listener, their own people, anyone else. `NPC.talked` (per listener reference: event keys and the day, the newest 40; saved, optional) keeps news from being told again in full: a grief or the listener's harm is touched on at most once a day, then "nothing new"; a witness's accusation is said in full once a day. The only state talking changes besides "met".
- **Tests:** `game/tests/dialogue_perspective_test.gd::test_merge_and_priority`, `game/tests/dialogue_perspective_test.gd::test_said_history`, `game/tests/dialogue_perspective_test.gd::test_gift_thanks`

### INT-421 · A player's crime, chase and killing are that player's (GP-CRIME-PURSUIT A8)
- **Trigger:** A guest (or the host) strikes or kills a villager, a bandit or a keeper's animal, in a world with several players.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/world.gd::kill`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_assault`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::_player_hit_npc`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::continue_as_heir`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::foe_player`, `game/scripts/core/relations.gd::slain_by_player`, `game/scripts/core/relations.gd::killings_of`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::by_tag`, `game/scripts/core/event_talk.gd::violence`, `game/scripts/core/event_talk.gd::person_of_id`
- **Affects:** Crime, Combat, Judgment, Save
- **Expected:** The record, the case, the guard's pursuit, the victim's fighting back and the blows all name the player who did it (`NPC.target_ref`, the pursuit's ref; `NPCBrain.foe_player`); the killing counts against that player (`Relations.guest_killings`, optional save key; notoriety, courtship and marriage refusals read the acting player's own). `World.kill` knows a player's killing by the hand's reference, never by the cause's first name (a villager or bandit sharing it is not the player). Judgment facts of a player carry "by:<player ref>" (judgment keys every player as -2 until MP Batch C), so the talk says "you" to the one who did it and names them to anyone else. The heir: only those on the dead player's case or fight let it go.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/manhunt_test.gd::test_two_players`, `game/tests/manhunt_test.gd::test_name_collision`

### INT-422 · A guest's character is kept by the host's world: away, reloaded, dead and succeeded (MP Batch B)
- **Trigger:** The host loads its save; a guest disconnects while a fighter or a guard is after it; a guest's character dies; its player chooses the heir (death screen, the request "heir").
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/world.gd::suspend_guests`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_load_save`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::fight`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::continue_as_heir`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_ref`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_of_ref`, `game/scripts/core/credit.gd::on_player_died`, `game/scripts/core/contracts.gd::on_player_died`, `game/scripts/core/manor_work.gd::release_player`, `game/scripts/core/identity.gd::succeed`, `game/scripts/net/net_actions.gd::_heir`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::apply_requests`, `game/scripts/main.gd::adopt_heir`
- **Affects:** Player state, Save, Combat, Crime, Coins, Employment, Inheritance, Family
- **Expected:**
  - A loaded world has every guest out of play (`PlayerState.away`, MP-D1) until it rejoins, whatever the save said (a save made while it played); every record stays. A fighter whose player target is away gives up the fight; a guard on its case cannot see it (`World.sees`), so the chase becomes a search and the case stays wanted.
  - A guest's death waits for its player (MP-D4): the world goes on and nothing chooses for it. "Continue as the heir" runs the established succession (`World.continue_as_heir`) as that guest, for its line only: its own debts and claims, contracts and manor job (by the acting player's reference); since MP Batch C (INT-423) the links to the line's person id are retired (the host's to -3, a guest's to the dead character's own pid) and the line's ties are recorded in `tie_history`, for whichever line died. The heir carries the identity hash (`net_key`) and the line (`PlayerState.line`, the first guest character's pid; save key `line`, written only when set), so the line keeps the reference `"player:<line>"` across generations as the host's line keeps `"player"`: what it holds and owes, its killings, stay the family's; the case ended with the dead. The heir's starting purse is counted in `coin_sources`.
  - Single player: no guests, the host's heir as before (`"player"`, no line, no new keys).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/multiplayer_b_test.gd`, `game/tests/net_batch_b_test.gd`


### INT-423 · Every player is their own person: ties, family, judgment, foes and reports by player (MP Batch C)
- **Trigger:** Any player acts on or near a villager (talk, a gift, help, trade, a blow, a theft, a killing, courting, a partnership, a birth); a villager judges, fights, reports or tells of a player; the world reads a player's notoriety.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/life.gd::is_player_id`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::person_id`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::acting_id`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_of_id`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::id_of_ref`, `game/scripts/core/relations.gd::notoriety`, `game/scripts/core/relations.gd::player_tick`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::partner_player`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::births`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::retire_player_id`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_live.gd::by_tag`, `game/scripts/core/judgment/judgment_bonds.gd::_pk`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::foe_player`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::report_crime`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::deliver_report`, `game/scripts/core/event_talk.gd::person_of_id`, `game/scripts/core/contracts.gd::ref_of`
- **Affects:** Family, Judgment, Crime, Combat, Market, Inheritance, Player state
- **Expected:**
  - A player's person id stands for them wherever a villager's id would: the host's line -2 (ID-D1, every save), a guest's line the negative of its line's pid (`World.person_id`); `"player:<line>"` and `-line` name one line. Each player's ties, Good and Bad ledgers, facts, partner and children's links, the villagers fighting them and the reports on them are their own: one player's deed is never another's (MP-D7: a newcomer starts with none). The existing rules read them unchanged; with one player the id is -2 everywhere (single player unchanged, G0).
  - A guest heir keeps its line's id, so the village's view of the family passes to the heir as the host's does; the dead's links name the dead person.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/multiplayer_c_test.gd`, `game/tests/net_c_test.gd`, `game/tests/manhunt_test.gd`

### INT-424 · A guest's verbs are applied by the host; the guest's copy of the world follows (MP Batch C)
- **Trigger:** A guest's interface calls a player verb (any of `NetActions.VERBS`); the host applies a request; every few seconds.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/world.gd::mirror`, `game/scripts/net/net_actions.gd::apply_verb`, `game/scripts/net/net_actions.gd::enc`, `game/scripts/net/net_actions.gd::dec`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::send_verb`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::send_syncs`, `game/scripts/net/net_sync.gd::changed`, `game/scripts/net/net_sync.gd::adopt`, `game/scripts/main.gd::adopt_own_state`
- **Affects:** Player state, Inventory, Coins, Market, Property, Crime, Interface, Save
- **Expected:**
  - On a guest's machine the outermost verb an action calls is sent to the host with its arguments named as both machines name them, and run locally only so the interface answers at once. The host applies the same verb as that guest (`World.as_actor`) with its own rules, refusing an unknown verb, an argument that names nothing there, another player's reference, person id, character or store, and what is beyond the guest's reach. Requests are applied once, in arrival order, at one point of the host's frame (two players reaching for one thing: the first gets it).
  - The answer carries the guest's character as the host has it; the sections of the shared world whose hash changed since that guest last had them follow (after the request, then every 3 s), and the guest's copy takes them in place. The guest never simulates or saves; coins and goods exist only on the host.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/net_c_test.gd`, `game/tests/multiplayer_c_test.gd`

### INT-425 · Rest by agreement and walking in the core (MP-D2, MP Batch C1)
- **Trigger:** A player rests while another player in play is awake; the last one rests; a resting player moves, acts or is struck; any player steps.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_rest`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_wake`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::_update_one_player`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::damage_player`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::step_ok`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::try_step`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::move_ok`

One player: the rest of Build 19. Walking and its collision (the feet rule) are the core's, the rule the host checks a guest's steps by.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/multiplayer_c_test.gd`, `game/tests/net_c_test.gd`

### INT-426 · What each player holds and owes is their own: stations, stalls, shops, carts, purses by reference (MP Batch C2-C4)
- **Trigger:** A player reserves a station; the day's rent and wages; the hour's attendant and buyers; a cart stop delivery; any rule that pays, charges or reads a player by reference while someone else acts.

- **Affects:** Property, Market, Coins, Workshops, Containers
- **Expected:** Each player holds a station under their own key; a stall, a shop (`shop.by`), a cart, a lease and a debt are their holder's; every helper that takes a reference reaches that player's purse, pack or chest (`World.player_for`), whoever is acting. The world's own work (rent, wages, attendants, buyers, the census, decay) covers every player's premises. One player: as before.

### INT-427 · Animals and bandits choose among the players (MP Batch C6)
- **Trigger:** A player strikes a wolf or bear; a hungry beast looks for someone alone; prey flees; a bandit stalks at night.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/fauna.gd::provoke`, `game/scripts/core/fauna.gd::_foe_pos`, `game/scripts/core/fauna.gd::_hunt_people`, `game/scripts/core/fauna.gd::_bite`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_prey_for`
- **Affects:** Wildlife, Bandits, Combat, Health

### INT-428 · The hue and cry travels from voice to voice (owner decision 2026-10-24)
- **Trigger:** A witness of a killing, a guard struck, a pursuer who sees violence again raises the hue and cry; a second known killing rings the bell.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::hue_and_cry`
- **Affects:** Crime, Combat
- **Expected:** The cry is heard by those awake within earshot of the voice (`witness_radius`, the same building or the open air, the same level); each villager who hears it takes it up where they stand, until it dies away `hue_and_cry_tiles` from where it was raised. Nobody out of earshot of every voice hears it. The bell is heard within `bell_tiles` of where it is rung, through walls; it is not rung on. Every guard who hears comes; every villager who hears learns who is cried after.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/manhunt_test.gd`

### INT-429 · Between two players both must agree (MP-D8)
- **Trigger:** A player beside another offers to be partners or a gift (the players panel: Use beside them); the other accepts or declines; the day ends.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_offer`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_answer`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::players_partner_refusal`, `game/scripts/ui/game_ui.gd::show_players`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::found_couple_household`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::ensure_couple_households`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::sync_couple_homes`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::births`, `game/scripts/core/life.gd::retire_player_id`, `game/scripts/core/guardianship.gd::orphans`, `game/scripts/core/guardianship.gd::player_household`, `game/scripts/core/property.gd::_anyone_alive`, `game/scripts/core/construction.gd::daily`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::household_stores`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::migrate_22_to_23`, `game/scripts/core/save_transfer.gd::validate`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::players_list`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::s_players`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::public_player`
- **Affects:** Family, Households, Inventory, Interface, Save
- **Expected:** Nothing is done until the other accepts, and it is checked again then (both beside each other, both able). Partners: both living adults of partnering age, neither partnered, not kin; each link names the other's person id; no feast. Accepted, the pair founds its couple household (MP-PLAYER-FAMILY, Build 22, save 23: `Life.found_couple_household`; a household record with `couple`, the two person ids, the host's line first; members are villagers only, the couple's children; home: the first of them with a house, `Life.sync_couple_homes` at dawn and on load). In it a married man and woman (the father in play or away) have children by `Life.births`' own rules (mother's age, food counted over the household and the two players' packs and chests, the interval, the daily chance) when it has a house, both recorded as parents and the child in each spouse's `children`; a couple of one sex never has a birth. Any married couple may take an orphan in by its own act (`Guardianship.player_adopt`, INT-166): `NPC.adoptive` names both spouses, mother and father stay as born, no new person. The household is cared for while either spouse lives (not orphaned, never ended, no housing goal, nobody lodged in it, no orphan placed in it by the village, its children never sent away by migration); a spouse's death names the dead as an ancestor in `couple`, `adoptive` and the links (`Life.retire_player_id`). A save 22's married players get their household on load (`World.migrate_22_to_23`, appended after the saved households; idempotent). The other players' partner and children reach a guest in the players snapshot and their public record. A gift moves the goods once, their stolen marks with them. Declined or lapsed at the day's end: nothing moves. Saved under `player_offers` (optional).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/multiplayer_c_test.gd`, `game/tests/player_family_test.gd`, `game/tests/net_family_test.gd`

### INT-430 · Save 22: a Build 20 save and a save 21 with guests read with nothing lost (MP Batch C)
- **Trigger:** Loading or importing a save 21 (Build 20, or Batch A-B with guests).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/world.gd::migrate_21_to_22`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::load_dict`, `game/scripts/core/save_transfer.gd::migrate`
- **Affects:** Save, Family, Judgment, Crime
- **Expected:** Everything a save 21 held is kept; the single player is the host by its permanent pid and -2 in person-id records (ID-D1); what save 21 kept under -2 stays the host's line's (a guest's ties and opinions then were everyone's and cannot be split), a guest's tagged facts still name it in talk; a villager's report on a guest names the guest's id. The step is idempotent; an older build refuses a save 22.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/multiplayer_c_test.gd`


### INT-431 · A guest's copy of the animals follows the host's (Build 21)
- **Trigger:** A guest is connected; four times a second (the snapshots), at a birth or a death on the host, after the guest's request and on the slow section timer.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::send_animals`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::s_animals`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::s_fauna_life`, `game/scripts/net/net_sync.gd::adopt_fauna`, `game/scripts/net/net_sync.gd::add_animal`
- **Affects:** Livestock, Wildlife, Interface
- **Expected:**
  - The host alone simulates the animals (the guest's `World.update` never runs). Each guest gets the live state of the animals within `NetProtocol.NPC_RANGE` of it (place, facing, walking, a one-off animation, health) four times a second, only what changed, and every one of them once a second; births and spawns (the whole record) and deaths, kills and removals anywhere, reliably and at once; the records and pens (health, hunger, area, trough, keeper, tenant, products, the players' own pens) as the "fauna" section of `NetSync`, after the guest's own request and on the timer with the villagers' records.
  - An animal near the guest keeps the snapshots' place; one beyond them takes the host's place, standing. An animal the host lacks is removed from the copy and its sprite fades; one the copy lacks is added in id order.
  - Single player sends nothing: no change to the simulation (G0).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/net_fauna_test.gd`, `game/tests/net_g1_test.gd`

### INT-432 · Opening a world to friends and joining one from the menus (Build 21)
- **Trigger:** The pause menu's Open to friends (Open, Stop hosting); the title screen's Join a game (Join, a found game, Cancel); a guest's Leave the game.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/ui/net_window.gd::show_host`, `game/scripts/ui/net_window.gd::show_join`, `game/scripts/ui/net_window.gd::parse_address`, `game/scripts/ui/game_ui.gd::show_pause`, `game/scripts/ui/game_ui.gd::compact`, `game/scripts/main.gd::host_game`, `game/scripts/main.gd::stop_hosting`, `game/scripts/main.gd::leave_guest_world`, `game/scripts/net/net_discovery.gd::lan_addresses`
- **Affects:** Interface, Save
- **Expected:**
  - Open calls `Main.host_game` (`NetSession.host`) with the password typed; its failure is shown in the window. Stop hosting calls `NetSession.stop` (guests go out of play, every record kept) and saves; Save and quit to title stops hosting first. A guest's menu has no Save (the host keeps the world) and leaves by `Main.leave_guest_world`.
  - Join calls `NetSession.join` with this device's identity key, the name and the password; the session's messages (refusals, a lost connection) show in the window; an attempt not welcomed within 20 s is given up. The name and address are kept in the settings file, never in a save.
  - Single player is unchanged unless the player opens the world (G0).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/net_ui_test.gd`, `game/tests/start_test.gd`, `game/tests/wiki_test.gd`

### INT-433 · Market stalls show the goods on offer on their shelves (GP-MARKET-SHELVES, Build 21)
- **Trigger:** Every half second while a world is shown (StallDisplayView.refresh).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/business.gd::shelves`, `game/scripts/core/business.gd::merchant_goods`, `game/scripts/core/business.gd::display_goods`, `game/scripts/view/stall_display_view.gd::_refresh_shelves`, `game/scripts/view/stall_shelf.gd::show_goods`
- **Affects:** Market, Interface
- **Expected:**
  - Every market stall (the merchants' stalls, the manor's pitches, built stalls) is drawn as a Craftpix shelf in the y-sorted actors node, its old tiles off the drawn map; collision, containers and map data unchanged.
  - A pitch or built stall shows its stall stock, a merchant's stall what the merchant at it offers (`NPCBrain.for_sale`, most held first) while the merchant is at the stall (`Stalls.is_open`), up to ten kinds, five a board; nothing when the stock is empty or the merchant away. Shops and storefronts keep their counter display.
  - Read only: no goods or coins move. A guest's machine computes the same shelves from the synchronized `stalls` and `npcs` sections and the villagers' snapshots (no new synchronization).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/market_shelf_test.gd`, `game/tests/business_works_test.gd`

### INT-434 · The smith sharpens a weapon at the grinding wheel for a coin (GP-SHARPEN, Build 21)
- **Trigger:** A player at the smithy's grinding wheel chooses Sharpen; `World.npc_sharpen` for a villager, guard or bandit.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_sharpen`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::npc_sharpen`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::sharpen_refusal`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::smith_at_grindstone`
- **Affects:** Inventory, Coins, Workshops, Equipment
- **Expected:** With a smith awake within 6 tiles of the wheel (village.json `spawns.grindstone`), a worn weapon's wear is mended to full (no damage bonus) for `World.SHARPEN_COINS` (1) paid from the payer's purse to the smith's (earned counted); refused with a reason when the weapon is whole or not a weapon, the smith is away, or the payer lacks the coin. Coins move between holders `World.total_coins` counts. A guest's request goes to the host (`NetActions.VERBS`). Villagers do not yet choose to sharpen on their own (no AI change: G0 identical).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/sharpen_test.gd`

### INT-450 · The player sees when the guards hunt them, and why (GP-WANTED-INDICATOR, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** Every HUD update and view frame while the local player has a case (`Justice.alert` of `World.acting_ref`).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/justice.gd::alert`, `game/scripts/ui/wanted_hud.gd::refresh`, `game/scripts/view/wanted_ring.gd::set_on`, `game/scripts/ui/game_ui.gd::update_hud`
- **Affects:** Crime, Interface
- **Expected:** Read only, no new rule: a red ring round the player's character while any guard is on the case (pursuing, responding or searching); a note under the status box naming the state (pursued, coming, searched for, wanted) and the gravest outstanding crime the guards know, its victim and place, never a guard or a guard's place. Nothing shows with no case, unknown crimes, settled or lapsed crimes, or a closed case. Per player: a guest reads its own case from the host's copy (NetSync "justice", with the npcs' pursuit state).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/wanted_indicator_test.gd`, `game/tests/wiki_test.gd`

### INT-451 · Worn armour is drawn on the character and on other players' avatars (ARMOR-01, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** A player puts on or takes off armour or a head item; a load; an heir; a player snapshot in a shared world.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/view/character_look.gd::apply_worn`, `game/scripts/view/character_look.gd::over_baked`, `game/scripts/view/character_look.gd::player_sheet`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_refresh_player_worn`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_sync_avatars`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::public_player`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::players_list`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::s_players`, `tools/import_game_assets.py`
- **Affects:** Character, Equipment, Interface
- **Expected:** Every armour and head item has a `worn_layers` entry (character_style.json, from the importer's WORN_LAYERS; plain clothes an entry with no layer); the player's sheet is the recipe with the worn layers, or a baked sheet with them laid over; taking an item off restores the sheet exactly; a load keeps what is worn. Other players' armor and head travel in the public record and the snapshots, and their avatars are redrawn when it changes. View only: the recipe and the save are unchanged.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/character_look_test.gd`, `game/tests/armour_worn_test.gd`

### INT-452 · Underground supports and the way out are drawn inside their cells (GP-MINE-WOOD-FRAME, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** A level below ground is drawn; a support is set up, worn or repaired; a shaft's ladder is set.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/view/works_view.gd::support_parts`, `game/scripts/view/works_view.gd::_draw_support`, `game/scripts/view/works_view.gd::_draw_ladder_foot`, `game/scripts/view/way_out_mark.gd::setup`, `game/scripts/view/level_view.gd::setup_level`
- **Affects:** Resources, Interface
- **Expected:** A timber support is a beam across the passage with its post heads, wholly inside its floor cell, so it never covers a seam beside it; a ladder's foot stays in its cell; each level reached from the surface shows its way out as daylight and steps at the portal's door. Placement, collision, access and mining unchanged.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/mine_frame_test.gd`
### INT-460 · Armour and weapons are made to order by their makers (ARMOR-CATALOG-01, WEAPON-CATALOG-01, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** The player orders a catalogue piece from the smith, the tanner or the tailor (talk window, GearWindow); a guest's order goes to the host as the verb `player_order_gear`.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/gear_orders.gd::terms`, `game/scripts/core/gear_orders.gd::order`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_order_gear`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::work_craft`, `game/scripts/ui/gear_window.gd::show`, `game/scripts/net/net_actions.gd`
- **Affects:** Crafting, Equipment, Inventory, Coins, Ledger, Interface
- **Expected:** The piece's own recipe is worked by the maker under World.work_craft (the maker's skill level, its xp, the hammer's wear at the anvil, a practised hand's saving kept by the maker, never a bonus unit of gear). A commission's `bring` materials come from the buyer's pack; the maker supplies the rest from its stock beyond what contracts hold, and what it lacks comes from the buyer's pack uncharged; the price is the fee plus the maker's units at base value, from the buyer's purse to the maker's (earned). All or nothing: a refusal (wrong maker, asleep or away, too little skill, materials short, coins short, a full pack) moves nothing. Coins and goods conserved; the craft counts used_ and made_ in the ledger.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/gear_catalogue_test.gd`

### INT-461 · Worn gear gives its bonus while worn (ARMOR-CATALOG-01, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** A piece with a `bonus` is put on, held or taken off; each player update; a blow; a fireball that lands.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/player_state.gd::bonus`, `game/scripts/core/player_state.gd::damage`, `game/scripts/core/player_state.gd::hp_cap`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::_update_one_player`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::player_fireball_hit`, `game/scripts/main.gd::swing_time`, `game/scripts/ui/inventory_window.gd::_build_details`
- **Affects:** Equipment, Health, Combat, Interface
- **Expected:** The sum over the six gear slots, each holding one item, so a piece counts once however many are carried: max_hp and max_stamina raise the caps, fire_damage is added to a fireball's damage after the Magic tier, stamina_regen to the regeneration a second, damage to a blow's weapon damage before the Combat multiplier. A catalogue weapon's attack_speed divides the swing time (1 for every older weapon). Taking the piece off removes it. The Pack names each bonus. Player only; villagers' combat is unchanged.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/gear_catalogue_test.gd`

### INT-462 · Boar tusks and antlers come from killed boars and stags (ARMOR-MATERIALS-01, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** A grown wild boar or a grown stag is killed by the player, a hunter, a poacher or a family defending itself (Fauna.kill).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/fauna.gd::kill`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_hunter`, `game/scripts/core/npc_brain.gd::_plan_sell_all`, `game/data/balance.json`
- **Affects:** Wildlife, Resources, Market, Ledger
- **Expected:** animals.boar.tusks (2) boar tusks from a grown boar of either sex, animals.deer.antlers_m (2) antlers from a grown male deer; none from young animals or does, none at the start or from any other source; counted made_boar_tusk and made_antler. Hunters sell them with their other goods to the goods merchant (OUTLETS, BUYS.merchant_goods, up to 10 each), who sells them on; they go into commissioned armour and the masterwork weapons.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/gear_catalogue_test.gd`, `tests/test_gear_catalogue.py`

### INT-463 · The weapon in hand is drawn and swung (WEAPON-VISIBLE-01, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** Every frame a character is drawn; the player's attack; a villager's blow (anim "sword"); another player's snapshot.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/view/actor_sprite.gd::wield`, `game/scripts/view/actor_sprite.gd::strike`, `game/scripts/view/actor_sprite.gd::npc_weapon`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_swing_begins`, `game/scripts/main.gd::net_anim`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_sync_avatars`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::players_list`, `game/scripts/net/net_session.gd::public_player`
- **Affects:** Equipment, Combat, Bandits, Interface
- **Expected:** The item in the weapon slot is carried at the side, point raised, in all eight directions while standing and walking, hidden lying, sitting, at work and dead; a blow plays the axe's swing (throw frames, the swing poses) with it, played through. Guards and bandits carry their best weapon; anyone fighting swings theirs; other players' weapons travel in the snapshots and a blow is sent as "sword". View only: combat timing and rules are unchanged (the swing time apart, INT-461).

### INT-464 · The weapon catalogue is generated and listed by kind (WEAPON-CATALOG-01, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** GameData loads; the encyclopedia is built; a station's craft window, the Pack, the Skills window or a business's order list is shown.
- **Source:** `tools/build_weapons.py`, `game/scripts/core/game_data.gd::ensure`, `game/scripts/core/wiki_gear.gd::add_pages`, `game/scripts/core/wiki.gd::_items`, `game/scripts/core/skills.gd::recipes_at`, `game/scripts/core/business_works.gd::recipes`, `game/scripts/ui/game_ui.gd::show_craft`
- **Affects:** Crafting, Interface
- **Expected:** weapon_types.json's table expands into 1,920 items and anvil recipes (game/data/weapon_catalogue.json, generated, appended after the hand-kept data so nothing before changes order). Every list that would name them one by one groups them instead: one encyclopedia page per type with a designs grid; the craft and order windows by kind, then metal and design; the Pack and Skills by count or kind; the player's business order lists leave them out. tools/check_resources.py checks them with everything else and leaves them out of its documents.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/gear_catalogue_test.gd`, `tests/test_gear_catalogue.py`

### INT-470 · Every business does its business at one desk: a short menu for trade, ringing for service and the courier (GP-BUSINESS-DESK, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** The player stands beside a business's desk facing it (or on its keeper's cell facing it) and uses it (E, A, the Use button, a tap or a click); a market stall's desk by its own cells.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/business_desks.gd::all`, `game/scripts/core/business_desks.gd::faced_all`, `game/scripts/core/business_desks.gd::of_business`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_find_target`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_desk_target`, `game/scripts/main.gd::use_option`, `game/scripts/ui/game_ui.gd::show_desk`, `game/scripts/core/counters.gd::summon`
- **Affects:** Market, Interface, Workshops, Settlements
- **Expected:**
  - The desks are read from the world's records, nothing new is saved: the tavern's counter, the counters of skilled trades (`Counters.KEEPERS`: the smithy's table with the open ledger, `armory_counter`, kept by the smiths; the sawmill's table with stools, `sawmill_counter`, station s11 of generator v15, kept by the carpenters), each kept market stall (merchants', service pitches, a rented pitch, a built stall), a storefront and a player's shop counter. A business with no drawn desk (the sage) keeps none.
  - A counter desk is offered only within `World.STATION_BESIDE` of it, facing it (the probe on its cell), and reached across it or from its keeper's side; never from behind a wall or facing away.
  - The menu (`GameUI.show_desk`) states who is at the desk and offers what the existing rules allow: the ordinary trade window with the keeper there (INT-041, INT-019; prices, stock and ownership unchanged), otherwise ringing for service (INT-041) or the merchant's goods at a stall left alone, still theft and flagged as such (INT-043, DVW-A12 R16); the courier where a booth stands at the desk (INT-471). Each choice is done exactly as Use on it was before (`Main.use_option`).
  - Actor-aware: "your own business" is the acting player's (a guest's own pitch is the guest's desk, not the host's).
- **Tests:** `game/tests/desk_test.gd`, `game/tests/world_test.gd::_ctr01_counters`

### INT-471 · A business's courier booth stands at its desk; the courier is reached from the desk's menu (GP-BUSINESS-COURIER, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** `CourierService.open_shop_booth` (a villager business's first posting, or the player choosing "Open a courier booth at this desk"); a save loaded (`CourierService.seat_at_desks`); the player using a desk with a booth.
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/settlement/couriers.gd::open_shop_booth`, `game/scripts/core/settlement/couriers.gd::seat_at_desks`, `game/scripts/core/settlement/couriers.gd::location`, `game/scripts/core/business_desks.gd::booth_at`, `game/scripts/core/business_desks.gd::booth_on_desk`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_desk_target`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::load_dict`
- **Affects:** Market, Settlements, Coins, Inventory, Save, Interface
- **Expected:**
  - A business's booth stands at its desk (`BusinessDesks.of_business`): a merchant's at the stall (as before), a smith's at the smithy's ledger table, never on a work station; a player's at their own stall, storefront or shop desk. A business recipient's address is its desk (`location`, "the desk of ...").
  - Goods a business sends wait in the booth at its desk and payment collected for it comes back there (INT-320); coins and goods are conserved.
  - At the desk, the courier is one of the menu's options (it opens the ordinary courier window for that booth); the booth has no separate prompt there.
  - An older save's booth at a work station (Build 21 put a smith's at the forge) is moved to the desk on load, its cell only; its store, coins, deposits and routes are unchanged. No save version change.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/courier_desk_test.gd`, `game/tests/courier_live_test.gd::test_shop_booths`

### INT-472 · A business's desk table shows what the business offers, and the tavern's tables the dishes it serves: a look, never a pickup (GP-TABLE-ITEMS, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** The village view refreshes (`StallDisplayView.refresh`, every half second); `World.show_tables` (hourly with the tavern's tables, and when the tavern's offer changes).
- **Source:** `game/scripts/core/business.gd::desk_previews`, `game/scripts/core/business.gd::desk_keeper`, `game/scripts/core/business_desks.gd::table_cells`, `game/scripts/view/stall_display_view.gd::refresh`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::show_tables`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::table_offer`, `game/scripts/core/world.gd::table_previews`, `game/scripts/main.gd::_container_target`
- **Affects:** Market, Containers, Interface
- **Expected:**
  - Each counter desk's drawn table (village.json station "show" cells, generator v16: either side of the tavern's and the smithy's ledgers, the sawmill's table, whose drawn plate is taken off) shows the goods its business offers, two kinds a cell: the tavernkeeper's, or the keeper serving a skilled trade's counter, else its first keeper (`NPCBrain.for_sale`, most held first, as the market's shelves, INT-433). A new good shows, a sold-out one goes; nothing for sale, nothing shown.
  - A tavern table with nothing on it still shows its dish while the tavern offers that dish; its prompt says the dish is served there and bought at the counter (an info prompt): nothing is taken from it. Food the keepers set out on a table (INT-042) is unchanged.
  - Read only: no goods or coins move; no desk table is a container. Nothing is saved.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/table_preview_test.gd`

### INT-473 · The market's stalls stand apart; a built stall keeps the same spacing; an older save's market moves to the new places (GP-MARKET-SPACING, Build 22)
- **Trigger:** A new world (village.json generator v16: `stalls` and `pitches`); a save loaded (`Business.add_pitches`, `Business.seat_market`, `CourierService.seat_at_desks`); a stall built (`Stalls.site_refusal`, `Stalls.find_site`).
- **Source:** `tools/build_village.py`, `game/scripts/core/business.gd::pitch_specs`, `game/scripts/core/business.gd::add_pitches`, `game/scripts/core/business.gd::seat_market`, `game/scripts/core/stalls.gd::site_refusal`, `game/scripts/core/settlement/couriers.gd::seat_at_desks`
- **Affects:** Market, Property, Save, Settlements, Interface
- **Expected:**
  - Two rows of five, 5 tiles apart along a row and 5 rows apart (the north row the manor's four pitches and stall5, the south row stall0-4), chosen from the 53 x 43 px shelf (INT-433): three walkable cells and over a tile of open ground between neighbours, a clear lane between a row's customers and the shelves behind. Ids, kinds, tenants, the tailor's and barber's pitches unchanged. Every customer cell is reached on foot; no stall on a road, a tree, a plant, a building or another object. Trees, boulders, plants and the ground are exactly as in generator v15.
  - A built stall stands at least `Stalls.SPACING` (5, 5) from every other stall (before Build 22: 3 tiles).
  - A save from before (stalls and pitches at the v15 places) has each market stall and pitch moved to its new place with its use cell, everything it holds kept (tenant, stock, prices, till, sales, session); a courier booth at a stall follows it. If a built stall stands on the new place, the old one stays and the map is made to agree (its old cells solid; the new place's art off and walkable). No save version change.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/market_spacing_test.gd`, `game/tests/market_shelf_test.gd`, `game/tests/stalls_test.gd`, `tests/test_village.py`


### INT-480 · Recenter draws the player at the centre of the play area the persistent HUD leaves clear (GP-ANDROID-RECENTER, Expansion 20)
- **Trigger:** Every frame the camera follows the player (main._sync_sprites); Recenter (the button, C, the right stick's press); a change of zoom, screen size or HUD (the touch columns shown or hidden).

- **Expected:**
  - The followed point is drawn at GameUI.play_centre: the centroid of the screen less the status box and, when shown, the touch columns (messages, the prompt, the wanted note and Recenter do not count). The camera stands off by view_shift, the offset divided by the zoom, so it holds at every zoom and screen shape; the player is never drawn under the status box or the buttons after Recenter.
  - The dead zone, looking around (two fingers, the wheel, the right stick), the village map's look-there and walking are unchanged. Presentation only: no simulation state, no save field.
- **Tests:** `game/tests/x20_android_ui_test.gd`, `game/tests/start_test.gd`

### INT-482 · The touch action buttons are compact discs with their captions, closer to the right edge (GP-ANDROID-ACTION-BUTTONS, Expansion 20)

- **Expected:**
  - GP-ACTIONBAR's two columns, their nine actions, order and functions are unchanged. Each button is its glyph (the same art, a 40-unit box) on a 48-unit disc drawn behind it, its caption under the disc; the whole button is the touch target, at least 58 x 60 (70 high on a phone). A mode that is on: gold rim and caption, the words ON / OFF kept.
  - The columns stand TOUCH_EDGE (4) from the right and bottom edges of the safe area (were 12), never in a cutout or under a system bar; Recenter and the messages step beside them as before.
